# path<TAB>exact text of the hook (one per AE hook in an upstream file; notes/ae-hooks.md says where each goes).
# A hook of several lines writes them joined by \n: each line is matched with its indentation trimmed, and the
# lines must follow each other. tools/test_ae_hooks.py checks every hook is present, and every line marked
# AE hook upstream is a line of one listed here. A setting row of port_config.c is marked on its opening line: its
# help text, up to the row's }, belongs to that hook (an AE row's, or AE's edit of an upstream row's text).
# player 1's controller assigned by the PC menus (profile pick, campaign start, quick profile) takes player 1's
# active profile (0.2.3; ae_input.c): a layout's d-pad rule follows the player whatever the order of the two calls.
# Only at the port's own three sites: the Xbox lobby keeps profiles by CONTROLLER index, and a hook inside
# player_ui_set_single_player_local_player_controller gave a pad another player's look sensitivity.

# rules ae_map_share_channel_rules.c): a host reached only at its game port (a tunnel, a forwarded port) cannot be
# reached on TCP 5153. A connection carries a channel id (0: a socket's): the read and the write go to the channel, the
# connect is done at once, the guest opens one to an AE host that says it takes it (waiting 2 s for its hello), and the
# host takes a machine's stream as a connection. ONE line is UPSTREAM'S OWN LINE WITH AN AE TERM ADDED:
